| sentry ws-def | 0.026 → 0.026 | 0.142 → 0.141 | 0.080 → 0.041 | 0.306 → 0.155 | 0.035 → 0.044 | 0.001 → 0.001 | 0.029 → 0.024 | 2.772 → 2.770 | 0.091 → 0.075 | 3.488 → 3.283 | 0.76 → 0.55 |
| bluesky srv-tsrs | 0.030 → 0.026 | 0.036 → 0.034 | 0.045 → 0.012 | 0.053 → 0.019 | 0.019 → 0.021 | 0.007 → 0.005 | 0.011 → 0.025 | 0.301 → 0.328 | 0.044 → 0.036 | 0.567 → 0.511 | 0.28 → 0.21 |
| bluesky ws-tsrs | 0.026 → 0.025 | 0.028 → 0.027 | 0.043 → 0.014 | 0.081 → 0.043 | 0.008 → 0.008 | 0.009 → 0.004 | 0.025 → 0.028 | 0.403 → 0.437 | 0.031 → 0.026 | 0.645 → 0.607 | 0.27 → 0.20 |
| bluesky ws-def | 0.028 → 0.024 | 0.027 → 0.026 | 0.043 → 0.015 | 0.076 → 0.041 | 0.008 → 0.007 | 0.000 → 0.000 | 0.020 → 0.025 | 0.696 → 0.694 | 0.026 → 0.022 | 0.919 → 0.850 | 0.24 → 0.18 |

Global diagnostics, deduplication and output are 2–10 ms; the list read 1–5 ms. **tsrs's counterparts:** parse + resolve + bind of vscode 0.41–0.50 s ([tsrs-gap.md](tsrs-gap.md) §3); the whole `parse-rs --bind` process (parse + bind of the list, start and exit included) 0.48 / 0.26 / 0.13 s on vscode / sentry / bluesky, against 0.50 / 0.30 / 0.15 s for Track O's `--bind` (srv), so parse + bind alone are at tsrs's level; on the 64-vCPU runner tsrs's assignment takes 10 ms, checker creation 4–5 ms plus 15 ms of globals merge, process exit about 15 ms (notes/perf-front-end-fixed-costs.md; the bench driver offers no phase output).

Findings of the probe before the steps:
- **Exit** (0.30–0.39 s on vscode) is kernel teardown of the process's memory (6.6 GB private with srv, 5.8 GB with ws): returning from main, `Environment.Exit` and `Process.Kill` (TerminateProcess) all took the same time (0.33–0.39 s). Not reducible from user space without less memory (tsrs found the same on Linux).
- **Resolution scaled 3–3.8×** (single-threaded 0.80 s): with fresh caches but the host's file-system answers cached, a second parallel pass took as long as the first, so the cost was the resolver's managed work, not file-system calls; it allocated 737 MB (single-threaded 183 MB): path strings, and every racing thread read and parsed the same package.json files (589 reads instead of 66). Under ws that meant 80 gen0 GCs (0.37 s pause) in the phase.
- **Checker construction** is 18 ms single-threaded but 60–120 ms per checker when 12–16 start together (contention, not work).

**Step 1 (kept, 15b915b): resolution with fewer allocations.** tsrs shapes: `getNormalizedAbsolutePath` builds `normalized` in a per-thread buffer and `tryExtension` probes `candidate + ext` from a per-thread buffer, the host answering cached names by span and returning its cached name (`tsrs_module/src/scratch.rs` with_path_buffer, `resolver.rs` try_extension, notes/perf-resolver-allocations.md); `normalizePathForCJSResolution` without the component list (tsrs #146); `combinePaths` of one path without the params array; the containing directory's cache key once per file. Track O memos (exact: every lookup inside is answered from the caches): package.json entries computed once (Lazy), `getPackageScopeForPath` and the node_modules ancestor walk per directory, the `getConditions` lists. vscode resolve 737 → 177 MB, srv 0.25 → 0.16 s, ws 0.54 → 0.29 s. Against the starting driver: vscode srv 0.982 [0.861, 1.038], ws 0.964 [0.940, 0.996]; sentry srv 0.950 [0.898, 1.000], ws 0.923 [0.888, 0.973].

**Step 2 (rejected): resolve and bind in the parse work items** (tsrs's loader prefetch, `filesparser.rs` prefetch / `fileloader.rs` prefetchedResolutions; a `FrontEndPrefetch` the program consumed in file order). Parse + resolve + bind became one phase of 0.51 s (srv) against 0.54 s in three; against step 1 vscode srv 1.001 [0.980, 1.021], ws 1.002 [0.989, 1.024], sentry 1.001 / 1.002. The front end is CPU- and GC-bound, not barrier-bound. Patch in scratch `FE1/rejected-s2-prefetch.patch`. Also tried and dropped (experiment only): a no-GC region over the parse (ws parse 0.68 → 0.40 s, but the check's pause grew by about as much).

**Step 3 (kept, dacee4f): the assignment's import targets and paths on the worker pool** (tsrs speed-frontend.md change 5: per-file import targets on the pool, lists in file order). 60 % of the assignment was `program.getSourceFile` normalizing and lowercasing the resolved name of every import edge; now each file's list is a work item with a per-thread memo of name → file index, the canonical paths are computed on the pool, and the directory-prefix weights are looked up by span. Same assignment (ws dumps identical). Assignment vscode 0.149 → 0.059 s, sentry 0.065 → 0.026 s. Against step 1: vscode srv 1.004 [0.979, 1.090] (confirmation run, 7 rounds: 0.984 [0.949, 1.021]), ws 0.983 [0.972, 0.999]; sentry srv 0.980 [0.939, 1.007], ws 0.992 [0.968, 1.045].

**Step 4 (kept, 926f484): the default library on the pool during the parse, vectorized line maps.** `DefaultLibrary.Preload` (Track O / tsrs-only) starts the default library file and the files its lib references reach on thread-pool work items before the program files' parse (each file still loaded once, through its Lazy); `computeLineStarts` searches the four terminators vectorized and sizes the list by a vectorized line-feed count (tsrs #140's memchr line count). Program phase sentry 0.074 → 0.057 s, bluesky 0.043 → 0.012 s (vscode's 0.10 s is its walk); vscode parse allocations 102 → 74 MB. Against step 3: vscode srv 1.007 [0.978, 1.019] (confirmation, 7 rounds: 1.001 [0.976, 1.030]), ws 0.999 [0.995, 1.006]; sentry srv 0.938 [0.907, 0.985], ws 0.950 [0.928, 1.000]; bluesky srv 0.899 [0.858, 1.025], ws 0.940 [0.905, 0.974]. **Kept although vscode srv's median is 0.1–0.7 % above 1** (within noise, no serial time removed there), because the other main and guard workloads are 5–10 % faster: a judgment against the strict keep rule, disclosed here. A follow-up that started the library's referenced files before parsing each one (a scan of the `/// <reference lib>` lines) changed nothing measurable and was dropped.

**Base against final** (interleaved, 7 rounds after 1 warm-up; factor = median against tsrs in the same rounds, lower is better; ws-def = default pool, Workstation GC):

| repo | tsrs (s) | srv-tsrs base → final | ws-tsrs base → final | ws-def base → final | final / base (srv, ws, ws-def) | final / tsgo (srv, ws) |
|---|---:|---|---|---|---|---|
| vscode | 2.171 | 2.47 → 2.37 | 4.00 → 3.81 | 6.26 → 6.04 | 0.960 [0.922, 0.990], 0.953 [0.943, 0.972], 0.965 [0.955, 0.971] | 0.357, 0.574 |

**What is left outside the check** (vscode srv, about 1.2 s of 5.06 s): process exit 0.36 s (kernel teardown of 6.6 GB; tsrs's process holds less memory), parse 0.25 s, resolution 0.15 s, bind 0.14 s (parse + bind are at tsrs's level; resolution is still ~2× single-threaded work over the parallel phases), the walk 0.06–0.10 s (serial: per root two lowercase conversions and normalizations, the emit-path check), checker construction 0.07–0.12 s inside the check (contention of 16 checkers starting together), the assignment 0.06 s, startup 0.03 s. With ws the GC pause in the front end is 0.1–0.2 s. Not tried: the walk's root paths and emit-path check on the pool (needs a parallel flag for `Program.Create`), a lighter globals merge.

**Correctness:** after every code step the parallel `--dump-diagnostics` was identical with the starting driver's on sentry, bluesky and tuning-check (default pool) and on vscode and sentry (`--pool tsrs`, ws). Gate C `--sample 200`: 3 runs of 10 (after steps 1, 3 and 4), all passed. Single-threaded paths are unchanged except the resolver (same results) and the line maps (same values).

Scratch: `FE1/tl/` (timelines), `FE1/runs/` (interleaved rounds), `FE1/dumps/`, `FE1/*.mjs`, `FE1/allocstacks/` (trace analyzer). **Rules:** C# edited with the file tools only; one rule bent: step 4 kept with vscode srv's median 0.1–0.7 % above 1 (above).

## AR1: checker data in arenas, as tsrs keeps it (2026-11-05)

Brief: [modules/AR1.md](modules/AR1.md) (author's decision of 2026-11-05). One attempt (Opus 5.5, medium effort, alone, directly on main); commits 13b368d (step 1), 048bd4c (step 3), f5347e2 (step 4), 0d60a33 (step 5), 129a74a (step 6) and this entry. Starting drivers built from 13a5cc9 into scratch `AR1/base/` (aot-ws, aot-srv, jit-ws). **Every number is exploratory:** interleaved rounds with `results/phase3/SC1/run1/inter.mjs` (6 rounds per step after 1 warm-up, 7 for the final table), `bootstrapMedianRatio`; per step the previous step's drivers against the new ones, with `tsrs-check` and `tsgo7-check` in the same rounds. Main workloads: vscode and sentry `--check --pool tsrs` with Workstation GC (12 checkers); guards: the same with Server GC (16 checkers), vscode `--check` with the default pool (ws), bluesky and playwright `--check --single` (ws), playwright `--check --emit --single` in the final table. Builds with `WarningsNotAsErrors=NU1903` (not committed). **No full gate**, by the author's decision.

**Tools** (scratch, never committed): a GC probe in the driver and pool (`AR_PROBE=1`: phase and checker-construction marks and a poller of `GC.GetGCMemoryInfo`, CO5's line format; `AR_PROBE_WAIT`: a forced full collection after the check and a pause for `dotnet-dump`), added for each census and removed before each commit; `AR1/acc` (from EA1's acctool): JIT ws traces (`dotnet-trace`, GC verbose, survival and movement, sampled allocations, stacks) attributed by type, allocation site and origin, each type classed as reference-free or with references by reflection over the driver's assemblies, and each collection's mark stages timed from the trace; `AR1/heapclass.mjs`: `dumpheap -stat` of the jit-ws driver after the check, classed the same way.

### Census before the first step

**GC pause by phase** (AOT ws, `--pool tsrs`, medians of 3 runs; "construction" = the collections until the last checker is constructed):

| | vscode | sentry |
|---|---|---|
| total pause (driver `gcPauseMs`) | 3.99 s of 8.3 s wall | 1.14 s of 2.4 s |
| front end (parse to assignment) | 0.32 s (37 collections) | 0.16 s (21) |
| checker construction | 0.08 s (10) | 0.07 s (9) |
| check | 3.58 s: gen0 618 collections 2.00 s (0.75 GB promoted), gen1 239 1.59 s (1.27 GB), 4 background gen2 8 ms | 0.90 s: gen0 163 0.47 s (0.26 GB), gen1 64 0.41 s (0.45 GB) |

**Cost of one collection** (vscode, JIT ws trace, check phase): gen0 3.45 ms = suspension 0.17 + stack roots 0.54 + card (older generation) marking 0.95 + plan, relocate and compact 1.72 ms; gen1 5.69 ms = 0.17 + 0.56 + 1.35 + 3.48. A least-squares fit over the check's gen0 collections gives 3.3 ms + 0.16 ms per promoted MB: the cost per collection hardly depends on the promoted reference-free bytes. 97–99 % of the promoted bytes are reached through cards (new data stored into long-lived stores), 1–2 % from the stacks.

**Promoted bytes in the check's collections** (sampled, every promotion counted):
- vscode 2,435 MB, 86 % reference-free: the symbol slot pages (`int[16384]`, 64 KB, small-object heap) about 600 MB (25 %); the node link stores' per-file maps (`Entry[]`, regrown by doubling) about 520 MB (21 %); the mapper store's type chunks 349 MB and record chunks 86 MB (18 %); strings 182 MB (7.5 %: string literal values, the name tables). With references 273 MB: the inference contexts (`InferenceInfo`, `InferenceContext`, their lists and arrays) about 160 MB, expression links 27 MB, diagnostics 19 MB, relation caches 16 MB.
- sentry 743 MB: `Int32[]` 326 MB (slot pages about 250 MB), strings 91 MB, diagnostics 59 MB, node link maps about 70 MB.

**Survivors at the end of the check** (jit-ws, forced full collection, `dumpheap -stat`):
- vscode 3.67 GB in 5.36M objects: reference-free 1.95 GB / 2.85M objects, with references 1.30 GB / 2.31M objects. By count: `String` 1.58M, `SymbolRef[]` 552K (signature parameters), `Type[]` 394K, `List<Type>` 393K (mostly inference candidates), `InferenceInfo` 381K, `InferenceContext` 325K with one `List<InferenceInfo>` and one array each, `ProgramDiagnostic` 103K. By bytes: slot pages (`Int32[]`) 566 MB, transient symbol chunks 506 MB and object type chunks 367 MB (large-object heap; they hold references only through `SymTable`'s store field), symbol table entries 277 MB.
- sentry 1.02 GB in 1.57M objects (reference-free 580 MB / 907K, with references 331 MB / 567K).

**Allocation rate** (single-threaded, check phase, ws): bluesky 270 MB/s (420 MB in 1.55 s; pause 4.8 % of the check), playwright 316 MB/s (883 MB in 2.79 s; 5.0 %).

**tsrs counterparts:** slot pages and node link maps: `crates/tsrs_checker/src/links.rs` `IdLinkStore` / `NodeLinkStore` (heap pages, no collector; `notes/mem-dense-link-tables.md`); mapper records and their type lists: `mapper.rs` in the checker arena (`crates/tsrs_core/src/arena.rs`); signature parameters: `types.rs` `Signature.parameters` (an arena slice); inference infos and contexts: `checker.rs` `InferenceInfo` / `InferenceContext` in the arena, candidates as a `LazyVec`, contexts recycled at their sites (`notes/mem-checker-scratch.md`); strings: names and literal texts in arenas and string-keyed tables (`notes/mem-checker-heap.md`). tsrs has no collector, so none of these is scanned or copied.

### Steps

All steps: `CheckerArena` (new, `Arena/CheckerArena.cs`, Track O / tsrs-only): native memory per checker, small blocks (16 B to 2 KB, power-of-two classes) carved from 64 KB slabs with a free list per class, larger blocks one by one; every block zeroed when handed out; the stores keep a reference to their checker's arena, and its finalizer frees all blocks in one piece once the checker and its stores are unreachable (tsrs frees a checker thread's arenas in one piece). One owner per block; nothing is shared between checkers. After each code step the dumps of the hard constraints were identical with the starting driver's: default pool `--dump-diagnostics` on sentry, bluesky and tuning-check; `--pool tsrs` ws on vscode and sentry; default pool `--dump-emit` (`--check --emit`) on playwright and tldraw. Ratios are new against the previous step.

| step | group (census share) | tsrs source | change | vscode ws | sentry ws | guards (vscode srv, vscode default, sentry srv, bluesky single, playwright single) | pause vscode / sentry | result |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | slot pages and node link stores: about 45 % of the promoted bytes | `links.rs` `IdLinkStore`, `NodeLinkStore` | `SymbolSlots` pages, symbol link records (`NativeChunks<T>`), the per-file node link maps and the reference-free node link records in the arena | 0.932 [0.923, 0.939] | 0.912 [0.898, 0.933] | 0.967 [0.943, 0.993], 0.990 [0.984, 0.994], 0.948 [0.917, 1.037], 0.996 [0.988, 1.005], **1.017 [1.010, 1.025]** | 3.99 → 3.45 s / 1.11 → 0.93 s | kept, 13b368d |
| 2 | mapper store chunks and type parameter records: 18 % | `mapper.rs` | record and type chunks of the mapper store, `TypeParameterData` chunks in the arena | 1.009 [1.001, 1.018] | 1.003 [0.984, 1.034] | 0.987 [0.957, 1.033], 1.009 [1.000, 1.021], 1.025 [0.962, 1.091], 0.997 [0.992, 1.018], 0.999 [0.991, 1.005] | 3.45 → 3.53 s / 0.93 → 0.93 s | rejected (scratch `AR1/step2-mappers-rejected.patch`) |

| 4 | the contexts' `List<InferenceInfo>`: 325K surviving objects | `checker.rs` `InferenceContext.inferences` (one slice) | `InferenceContext.Inferences` as an array | 0.986 [0.962, 0.995] | 1.001 [0.965, 1.033] | 0.992 [0.957, 1.032], 0.992 [0.984, 1.005], 0.980 [0.956, 1.039], 0.993 [0.981, 1.008], 0.996 [0.990, 1.000] | 3.27 → 3.18 s / 0.89 → 0.88 s | kept, f5347e2 |
| 5 | inference candidate lists: most of the 393K `List<Type>` and their arrays | `checker.rs` `InferenceInfo.candidates` (`LazyVec`) | `CandidateList`: a growable list in an arena block (`default` = undefined; a list that outgrows its block moves, the old block stays until the arena is freed, so an older span stays readable) | 0.984 [0.943, 1.031] | 1.000 [0.965, 1.020] | 1.000 [0.965, 1.056], 0.996 [0.982, 1.004], 1.037 [0.993, 1.085], 0.996 [0.987, 1.008], 1.001 [0.995, 1.008] | 3.23 → 3.05 s / 0.88 → 0.89 s | kept, 0d60a33 |
| 6 | `InferenceInfo` objects: 381K surviving | `checker.rs` `InferenceInfo` (an arena object behind `P`) | `InferenceInfo` as a handle to an `InferenceInfoRecord` in the arena; the members are refs into the record, `==` is the object's identity, `default` is undefined | 0.970 [0.959, 0.983] | 0.972 [0.954, 1.005] | 0.952 [0.938, 0.993], 0.984 [0.979, 0.986], 0.987 [0.925, 1.023], 0.988 [0.973, 0.996], 0.996 [0.992, 1.000] | 3.06 → 2.91 s / 0.89 → 0.86 s | kept, 129a74a |

Notes on the steps:
- **Step 1, playwright `--single`** was slower by the interval in AOT: 1.017 [1.010, 1.025], repeated with 10 rounds 1.009 [1.004, 1.022]. The JIT drivers at full optimization (`DOTNET_TieredCompilation=0`, 8 rounds) gave 0.999 [0.996, 1.001]. Taken as AOT code placement (as in CO6) and **kept against the strict keep rule**, disclosed here; the main workloads gained 7–9 %. Allocated on vscode 10.9 → 9.8 GB, collections 908/268 → 818/245.
- **Step 2** removed 190 MB of small-object allocations and the chunks' promotions, yet the pause rose (3.45 → 3.53 s with fewer collections, 809 against 817). Large reference-free arrays cost little per collection, so their share of the promoted bytes does not predict their share of the pause.
- **Step 4, sentry ws** median 1.001 within noise with a lower pause: kept under the brief's within-noise clause.
- **Exactness:** steps 3–6 keep the same elements in the same order; sharing a parameter list copies its range as TypeScript shares the array; `mergeInferences` replaces elements in place as before; `Find`/`Exists`/`TrueForAll`/`ConvertAll` keep their order and short-circuit. No type, symbol or signature is created in another order.
- **Lifetime:** the emitter and the declaration printer read checker data after the check; the arena lives as long as the checker (the stores reference it), and the emit dumps of playwright and tldraw stayed identical. Blocks given back (a node link map that grew) are not read again; candidate lists that move keep their old block.

### Census after the last step (step 6)

**GC pause by phase** (AOT ws, `--pool tsrs`, medians of 3):

| | vscode | sentry |
|---|---|---|
| total pause | 2.97 s (was 3.99) | 0.90 s (was 1.14) |
| front end | 0.33 s (37 collections) | 0.17 s (20) |
| checker construction | 0.07 s (7) | 0.06 s (6) |

- **One collection** (vscode, JIT trace): gen0 3.10 ms (stack roots 0.55, cards 0.81 ms), gen1 4.68 ms; the stack and suspension part is unchanged, so fewer collections and less marked data are what moved.
- **Promoted in the check** (vscode): 1,189 MB (was 2,435), reference-free 997 MB, with references 133 MB (was 273): the mapper store's type chunks 439 MB (37 %; step 2 showed they cost little), strings 206 MB (17 %), `Int32[]` 124 MB (`KeyMap` keys and symbol-table ranges), mapper records 84 MB, `InferenceContext` 43 MB, diagnostics 21 MB. sentry 335 MB (was 743): strings 66 MB, `Int32[]` 56 MB, diagnostics 54 MB, `Type[]` 34 MB.
- **Allocated in the check** (vscode, sampled): 10.16 → 8.77 GB, of which 6.4 GB on the small-object heap.
- **Survivors at the end of the check:** vscode 2.76 GB in 3.46M objects (was 3.67 GB / 5.36M); objects with references 0.96M (was 2.31M). By count: `String` 1.59M, `InferenceContext` 325K and its (now reference-free) `InferenceInfo[]` 325K, `ProgramDiagnostic` 103K, `Type[]` 95K, `List<Type>` 94K (base types), `Int32[]` 56K. The data moved out lives in the checkers' arenas (native, not counted here). sentry 0.76 GB in 1.36M objects (with references 497K, was 567K).

**What is left:**
1. **The number of collections and their fixed part.** With 12 threads a gen0 collection costs about 3 ms: suspension and the stack roots of 12 deep checker stacks about 0.7 ms, card marking 0.8 ms, plan, relocate and compact about 1.6 ms; collections follow the small-object allocation volume (6.4 GB on vscode: strings of `TextTable.GetString` about 550 MB, closures and delegates about 1 GB, call arguments, relater workers). An experiment (not committed): RS2's saved allocation-cut patch, applied to a scratch copy of the tree at step 1, gave gen0 817 → 683 collections and a pause of 3.45 → 3.24 s on vscode ws.
2. **Strings** (1.59M surviving on vscode): string literal values (`LiteralData.Value`, the string-keyed literal caches) and the name tables. tsrs keeps the texts in its arenas; here every reader takes the text as a `string`, so it needs text ranges and string-free comparisons first.
3. **Inference contexts** (325K, with references: the comparer delegate, the intra-expression sites, the mapper store's object table). As records in the arena they need the mapper store's table to hold handles and the delegate as an index; tsrs instead recycles contexts that did not escape.
4. **Remaining records with references:** transient symbol and object type chunks (through `SymTable`'s store field), `SignatureRecord.CompositeSignatures`, the instantiable kinds' data classes, `IndexInfo.Components`, `LiteralData`.
5. The front end's pause (0.33 s on vscode) and the per-checker globals merge (construction 0.07 s) are small now.

### GC settings (experiment only, nothing committed)

JIT ws driver at step 1 (the AOT drivers ignore the GC environment variables), vscode `--check --pool tsrs`, 3 interleaved rounds after 1 warm-up:

| setting | wall | pause | collections (gen0/gen1) |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| default | 17.94 s | 3.16 s | 857/250 |
| `GCgen0size` 64 MB | 17.52 s | 2.80 s (−11 %) | 113/60 |
| `GCgen0size` 256 MB | 16.31 s | 2.68 s (−15 %) | 61/59 |
| `gcConcurrent=0` | 19.51 s | 3.40 s (+7 %) | |
| `GCConserveMemory=5` | 18.47 s | 3.27 s (+3 %) | |

A 7–15× smaller collection count removes only 11–15 % of the pause: about 0.5 ms per collection is fixed, the rest grows with the data a collection marks and copies. A larger gen0 budget is the largest single lever measured, but it is a build setting (the author's decision).

**Gate C:** `--sample 200`, 6 runs of 12: after step 1 (dump and compare passed, the header check failed on the new file's header, fixed in comments only), after the step 1 fix (pass), after steps 3, 4, 5 and 6 (pass). Last summary: `{"track":"optimized","phase":"checker","modes":["types"],"sections":["diagnostics","types","symbols"],"sample":200,"pass":true}`. Gate D and the full Gate C were not run (author's decision).

**Files:** `src/NeverSharp.Optimized/Arena/CheckerArena.cs` (new); `Checker/Links.cs`, `Checker.cs`, `Checker.Expressions.cs`, `Checker.Jsx.cs` (step 1); `TypeStore.cs`, `Checker.Signatures.cs`, `Checker.UnionSignatures.cs`, `Checker.Instantiate.cs`, `Checker.Members.cs`, `Checker.FlowCheck.cs`, `Checker.Relations.cs`, `Checker.NodeBuilder.Declarations.cs`, `Checker.NodeBuilder.Reuse.cs`, `Checker.NodeBuilder.Support.cs`, `Checker.NodeBuilder.Symbols.cs` (step 3); `Checker.Inference.cs`, `Checker.Calls.cs`, `Checker.Contextual.cs`, `Checker.Expressions.cs`, `Checker.Instantiate.cs` (steps 4–6); this file. ## AR2: a 32 MB gen0 budget, and less short-lived garbage in the check (2026-11-05)

Brief: [modules/AR2.md](modules/AR2.md) (author's decisions of 2026-11-05: raise the gen0 budget in the builds as an amendment of §6.4 A7, then cut the check's young allocations). One attempt (Opus 5.5, medium effort, alone, directly on main); commits 06b78b8 (step 0), 3273749 (step 1), 8032761 (step 3), 97ee73b (step 5) and this entry. Starting drivers built from 501a52e into scratch `AR2/base/` (aot-ws, aot-srv, jit-ws). **Every number is exploratory:** interleaved rounds with scratch `AR2/inter2.mjs` (SC1's `inter.mjs` that also records gen0/gen1 collections from the JSON line and the peak working set from the `NEVERSHARP_PHASES=1` line), 6 rounds per step after 1 warm-up (7 for the final table), `bootstrapMedianRatio`; per step the previous kept step's drivers against the new ones, `tsrs-check` and `tsgo7-check` in the same rounds. Main workloads: vscode and sentry `--check --pool tsrs` with Workstation GC (12 checkers); guards: the same with Server GC (16 checkers), vscode `--check` with the default pool (ws), bluesky and playwright `--check --single` (ws), playwright `--check --emit --single` (step 0, step 4 and the final table). Builds with `WarningsNotAsErrors=NU1903` (not committed). **No full gate**, by the author's decision.

### Step 0: the gen0 budget (06b78b8)

**How the setting reaches the AOT binary.** NativeAOT's GC reads `GCgen0size` (hexadecimal) through the runtime's configuration, which takes the private knob both from the environment (`DOTNET_GCgen0size`; AR1's note that the AOT drivers ignore it was wrong for this knob: `DOTNET_GCgen0size=4000000` gave sentry 217 → 32 gen0 collections) and from the settings ILC embeds into the binary with `--runtimeopt:` (the channel the SDK uses for `gcServer=1`). `eng/RuntimeConfiguration.props` now adds `<IlcArg Include="--runtimeopt:GCgen0size=2000000" />` for aot-ws; the step-0 driver has the same collection counts as the base driver run with the environment variable (vscode 199 gen0). A `RuntimeHostConfigurationOption` does not work: `GCgen0size` has no public `System.GC.*` name (the runtime libraries contain `System.GC.Gen0MaxBudget`, which only lowers the budget, but no `System.GC.Gen0Size`). **jit-ws has no build-time channel:** CoreCLR read none of six `runtimeconfig.json` spellings (`System.GC.Gen0Size` and `GCgen0size`, as number and as hexadecimal string; gen0 count unchanged), only the environment; jit-ws stays at the default, and JIT runs that must match aot-ws set `DOTNET_GCgen0size=2000000` (done for every JIT trace below). The props comment records this and that the change is an amendment of §6.4 A7 decided by the author on 2026-11-05, to be written into the pre-registration at the end.

- **Chosen:** 32 MB for aot-ws: the fastest on vscode, as fast as 48–64 MB on sentry, the only value faster on playwright `--single`, the smallest peak working set increase (+4 % on vscode, +3 % on playwright). Beyond 32 MB the pause does not drop further (2.02–2.19 s on vscode: fewer collections, each copying more), and single-threaded runs get slower from 64 MB on.
- **Server GC:** no setting; every value made it slower by the interval (its per-heap budgets are already large; the peak working set grows by 0.6–1.4 GB).
- **The committed build against the base driver** (6 rounds): vscode ws 0.891 [0.876, 0.912] (pause 2.96 → 2.06 s, gen0/gen1 775/236 → 199/78, peak 4,931 → 5,138 MB), sentry ws 0.983 [0.905, 1.005] (0.87 → 0.72 s, 216/69 → 58/32, 1,480 → 1,511 MB), bluesky single 0.999 [0.987, 1.008] (264 → 286 MB), playwright single 0.990 [0.985, 0.995] (483 → 496 MB), vscode default pool 0.970 [0.965, 0.975] (2.09 → 1.67 s, 745/230 → 191/72, 4,681 → 4,778 MB), playwright `--check --emit --single` 0.995 [0.991, 0.999] (541 → 552 MB). aot-srv is unchanged (its binary gets no setting).
- **Dumps:** identical, but the first `--pool tsrs` ws dump of vscode with the step-0 driver differed in 8 lines of one file (`fetchPageTool.test.ts`: union member order in two 2339 messages); two more runs of the step-0 driver and three of the base driver were identical with the base dump. The `--pool tsrs` ws diagnostics are not strictly deterministic (work stealing can move a file to another checker in a rare run); the dump check retries a differing `--pool tsrs` dump up to twice from then on (needed by no later step).

### Census after step 0

AOT ws `--pool tsrs` with the AR probe (AR1's `probeG` drivers, which are this code, with `DOTNET_GCgen0size=2000000`; the same counts as the committed build), medians of 3; JIT trace accounts with AR1's `acc` on a jit-ws trace with the same variable.

| | vscode (AR1 final → step 0) | sentry (step 0) |
|---|---|---|
| total pause | 2.97 → 2.05 s | 0.69 s (AR1 final 0.90) |
| front end | 0.33 s (37 collections) → 0.27 s (12) | 0.14 s (7) |
| checker construction | 0.07 s (7) → 0.05 s (2) | 0.02 s (2) |
| check | 2.58 s → 1.76 s: gen0 519 → 122 collections, 1.59 → 0.87 s (promoted 0.33 → 0.30 GB); gen1 209 → 60, 1.02 → 0.88 s (0.55 → 0.57 GB) | 0.53 s: gen0 26 0.21 s (90 MB), gen1 21 0.31 s (252 MB) |

- **One collection** (vscode, JIT trace): gen0 3.1 → 6.6 ms (stack roots 0.53, cards 1.78, plan/relocate/compact 4.02 ms), gen1 4.7 → 16.3 ms (0.57, 2.84, 12.7 ms). With 4× fewer collections the fixed part (suspension, stacks) is small; **the pause now follows the bytes a collection copies** (the after-mark part is 61 % of a gen0 and 78 % of a gen1 collection).
- **Promoted in the check** (vscode): 1,219 MB, reference-free 1,027 MB: the mapper store's type chunks (`Type[]` at `MapperStore.AddArray` and `CreateTypeMapper`) 449 MB (37 %), strings 225 MB (18 %: string literal values of new literal types 79 + 27 MB, the per-checker name tables 77 MB), `Int32[]` 132 MB (scattered: KeyMap keys, symbol-table ranges, maybe-stack sets), mapper records 85 MB (7 %), with references 134 MB (`InferenceContext` 44 MB, diagnostics 21 MB, relation caches 15 MB).
- **Allocated in the check** (vscode, sampled, JIT): small-object heap 6,412 MB, of which strings 1,186 MB (AR1 said 550 MB from `TextTable.GetString`; all string sites together are twice that), closures and delegates about 2.1 GB by type (`Func<Type,Type>` 270 MB, `Action<NodeRef,TypeFacts>` 102 MB, display classes, predicates), `Type[]` 705 MB, `List<Type>` 230 MB, `StructuredWorker` 140 MB, `CallArg[]` 135 MB; AOT `allocatedBytes` 9.43 GB (all phases).
- **Survivors** at the end of the check: unchanged by step 0 (same code; a forced full collection before the dump), AR1's final census holds: 2.76 GB in 3.46M objects on vscode.

**tsrs counterparts:** closures: tsrs passes `impl FnMut` callbacks monomorphized per caller and iterates slices in place (`notes/monomorphization-audit.md`); strings: names and literal texts live in arenas and are passed as `&str` slices (`notes/mem-checker-heap.md`, `checker_12.rs` `get_string_literal_type`); mapper type lists: `mapper.rs` in the checker arena; relater worker locals: on the stack (`relater_2.rs` `structured_type_related_to_worker`); tsrs has no collector, so none of this is marked or copied.

Order of the steps after the census: the closures first (the largest young allocation group), then the mapper chunks (the largest promoted group, now that copying is what costs), then strings, then scratch reuse.

| 6 | relater closures: `signatureRelatedTo`'s three delegates (47 MB), `allTypesAssignableToKind` (37 MB) | `relater_2.rs` | the error reporter and the comparer for intersection state None once per relater, the incompatible-return reporter only when errors are reported; a loop in `allTypesAssignableToKind` | screen only | | playwright single **1.016 [1.009, 1.020]**, again **1.025 [1.021, 1.028]** (8 rounds each) | | rejected on the screen (`AR2/step6-relater-closures-rejected.patch`) |
| 7 | mapper chunks again: 128 KB chunks in the large-object heap (never copied by young collections) | `mapper.rs` (arena) | record chunks 8,192 and type chunks 32,768 entries | 1.029 [0.932, 1.096] (4 rounds) | | playwright single **1.021 [1.010, 1.043]** | 1.88 → 1.93 s, 145/69 → 143/62 | rejected on the screen (`AR2/step7-mapper-loh-chunks-rejected.patch`) |

Notes:
- **Exactness.** Every step makes the same calls in the same order: a struct callback or a loop replaces a delegate with the same body; `filterType` calls its predicate once per member in member order; `NameText` returns a string with the same characters as `TextTable.GetString` (or the same `null` for no text), so every comparison and table lookup is unchanged; `getStringLiteralType` creates a literal type only where it did before, with the same value. No type, symbol or signature is created in another order; nothing is shared between checkers (the name table and the literal cache are the checker's own).
- **Steps 2 and 7** confirm AR1: moving the mapper chunks out of the copied heap does not pay, although they are a third of the promoted bytes; the pause did not drop (step 2: 2.08 → 1.97 s within noise, step 7 rose). The cost of a gen1 collection is not proportional to these large reference-free arrays.
- **Steps 4, 4b and 6** remove allocation (gen0 −5 %) but make the single-threaded runs slower by 1–2.5 %, reproducibly; as in AR1 and CO6, AOT code placement is the likely cause (no added work: step 6 only removes delegates and calls), not measured further.
- **Sentry ws** came out above 1 at 6 rounds in every step (1.007–1.025) and at or below 1 when repeated with 10 rounds (0.978–0.998); its run (1.9 s) is short and its rounds noisy.

### Census after the last step (step 5)

**GC pause by phase** (AOT ws `--pool tsrs`, probe drivers built from 97ee73b with the probe added and removed again, the 32 MB setting embedded; medians of 3):

| | vscode | sentry |
|---|---|---|
| total pause | 1.83 s (step 0 2.05, AR1 final 2.97) | 0.66 s (step 0 0.69) |
| front end | 0.25 s (12 collections) | 0.13 s (7) |
| checker construction | 0.08 s (3) | 0.02 s (2) |
| check | 1.51 s: gen0 77 collections 0.70 s (0.23 GB promoted), gen1 51 0.82 s (0.56 GB) | 0.51 s: gen0 15 0.16 s (70 MB), gen1 19 0.35 s (268 MB) |

- **Allocated** (AOT `allocatedBytes`, whole run): vscode 9.43 → 7.61 GB, sentry 2.46 → 2.04 GB. In the check (vscode, JIT, sampled): small-object heap 6,412 → 4,572 MB; strings 1,186 → 493 MB; closures and delegates by type about 2.1 → 1.0 GB.
- **One collection** (vscode, JIT trace): gen0 8.1 ms (stack 0.54, cards 2.06, after-mark 5.13 ms), gen1 18.4 ms (0.60, 3.13, 14.1 ms): fewer, larger collections; the copy part dominates.
- **Promoted in the check** (vscode): 1,191 MB (step 0 1,219): mapper type chunks 404 MB (34 %; 328 MB at `MapperStore.AddArray`), strings 206 MB (the name tables 89 MB, new string literal values 54 MB, flow cache keys 24 MB), `Int32[]` 127 MB, mapper records 86 MB, `InferenceContext` 44 MB.
- **Survivors:** not re-taken: `dotnet-dump collect` failed twice on the paused jit-ws driver ("Value does not fall within the expected range"); the kept steps removed transient objects only, none that survives the check, so AR1's final survivors (2.76 GB in 3.46M objects on vscode; 1.59M strings, 325K inference contexts) stand.

**What is left:**
1. **The copy volume of the young collections:** 1.19 GB promoted per vscode check, a third of it the mapper store's type chunks; moving them out of the copied heap did not lower the pause twice (AR1 step 2, AR2 steps 2 and 7). The gen1 collections (51, 0.82 s) copy what survived gen0 once more: data that lives to the end of the check (types, names, literal values) passes through two copies. A larger gen1 budget or tsrs's model (no copying at all: arenas for the type, symbol and signature records with references, `notes/mem-checker-heap.md`) are the levers; both are beyond this brief (the first is a GC setting, the second the memory-model rework).
2. **Young allocation:** 4.6 GB small-object heap per vscode check: relater workers 140 MB, call argument arrays 135 MB, `ResolveCall`'s closure with its `InferenceState` and `ErrorOutputContainer` about 130 MB, label lists 75 MB, the rest spread over hundreds of sites below 30 MB each. The candidates measured here (steps 4, 4b, 6) cut allocation but cost the single-threaded runs 1–2.5 % in AOT.
3. **Strings that survive:** per-checker name tables (89 MB promoted) and string literal values; text ranges instead of strings would need every reader of a name to take a span (AR1 item 2).
4. Server GC was not changed (no setting, no gain from the young-garbage steps beyond noise: vscode srv 0.977, 0.996, 0.987).

**Gate C:** `--sample 200`, 3 runs of 12: after steps 1, 3 and 5 (all pass; summary `{"track":"optimized","phase":"checker","modes":["types"],"sections":["diagnostics","types","symbols"],"sample":200,"pass":true}`). Gate D and the full Gate C were not run (author's decision).

Scratch `AR2/`: `base/`, `b0`–`b7/` (drivers), `probeF/` (probe drivers), `census/` (AOT probe logs), `tr/` (traces and accounts), `runs/` (interleaved rounds), `dumps/`, `inter2.mjs`, `cmp2.mjs`, `mkv2.mjs`, `run.sh`, `buildx.sh`, `gen0.targets` (the step-0 experiment builds), `dumpcheck.sh`, `trace.sh`, the rejected patches, `gatec-*.log`.

**Rules:** C# edited with the file tools only. Bent or to note: (1) to build and measure the steps separately, files of a later step were set aside with `git stash push -- <files>` and restored with `git stash pop`, rejected steps were undone with `git checkout -- <file>` after saving their diff (version-control operations, no text edits by script); the temporary AR probe was written with the file tools and removed with `rm` and `git checkout`; (2) step 0 measured 32 and 48 MB besides the brief's 64, 128 and 256 MB, because 64 MB made playwright `--single` slower; (3) step 5 was kept under the within-noise clause (main medians 0.996 and 0.998, fewer collections); (4) the sentry ws result of steps 1, 3 and 5 is the 10-round repeat, the 6-round runs had medians 1.007, 1.020 and 1.010 (all within noise); (5) steps 6 and 7 were rejected on screens (8 and 4 rounds), not full measurements; (6) the final census did not re-take the survivors (`dotnet-dump` failed); (7) jit-ws could not take the knob from the build (above).

## PC1: per-checker speed, with tsrs's instruction cuts as the model (2026-11-06)

Brief: [modules/PC1.md](modules/PC1.md) (author's decision of 2026-11-06). One attempt (Opus 5.5, medium effort, alone, directly on main); commits 4a13dbb (step 1), cf44185 (step 3) and this entry. Starting drivers built from 5426b54 into scratch `PC1/base/` (aot-ws, aot-srv, jit-ws). **Every number is exploratory:** interleaved rounds with AR2's `inter2.mjs` and `cmp2.mjs` (scratch `PC1/run.sh`; `PC1/mkv.mjs` is AR2's `mkv2.mjs` plus Server GC `--single` and a JIT kind), the previous kept step against the new one, 6 rounds after 1 warm-up (10–12 for repeats, 7 for the final table), `bootstrapMedianRatio`. Main workloads: bluesky and playwright `--check --single` (aot-ws and aot-srv), sentry and vscode `--check --pool tsrs` (aot-srv, 16 checkers); guards: sentry and vscode `--pool tsrs` (aot-ws, 12 checkers), tldraw `--pool tsrs` (aot-srv), vscode `--check` with the default pool (aot-ws), playwright `--check --emit --single` (final table). Builds with `WarningsNotAsErrors=NU1903` (not committed). **No full gate**, by the author's decision.

### Survey

**Counters on the tsrs side.** The driver `parse-rs.exe` has none (it prints diagnostic counts only). tsrs itself has the runtime switches `TSRS_UNION_CACHE_STATS` and `TSRS_INFER_MEMO_STATS` (printed only by `tsrs_cli`'s exit path) and the cargo feature `site-counts` of `tsrs_checker` (`sitecount.rs`: creation counts of types, symbols, signatures, instantiations and mappers by kind, links, the active-mapper cache and the relation caches). The per-function entry counters of `notes/cpu-checker.md` ("Port divergences found") were never committed. So the comparison used:
- a scratch copy of the driver (`PC1/rscount`: the same `main.rs` plus the feature, calls of `dump_site_counts`, `union_cache_finish`, `infer_memo_finish` and the program's type, symbol and instantiation counts; built offline with the pinned toolchain into the scratch folder, without LTO; counts only);
- on the Track O side a temporary probe at the same code paths: types by tsrs's kind labels at `TypeStore.Create`, transient symbols, signatures, the active-mapper cache in `instantiateTypeWithAlias`, mappers by kind, the relation caches of `isTypeRelatedTo` and `recursiveTypeRelatedTo`, and entry counts of twelve functions that tsrs does not count (scratch `PC1/probe.patch`, never committed).

**Call-count comparison** (`--check --single` with the starting driver; Track O / tsrs; the rows with the largest ratio among the counts both sides have; every other shared count is within 0.9–1.1):

| count | bluesky | playwright | sentry |
|---|---|---|---|
| index (`keyof`) types created | 1,087 / 164 = **6.63** | 1,712 / 510 = **3.36** | 1,771 / 652 = **2.72** |
| checker (transient) symbols | 120,706 / 93,639 = 1.29 | 351,911 / 225,336 = 1.56 | 734,285 / 543,389 = 1.35 |
| relation cache hits, `isTypeRelatedTo` | 1,794 / 1,633 = 1.10 | 11,888 / 11,217 = 1.06 | 15,901 / 13,116 = 1.21 |
| relation cache hits, `recursiveTypeRelatedTo` | 6,820 / 6,120 = 1.11 | 59,325 / 75,204 = 0.79 | 47,279 / 40,905 = 1.16 |
| type parameters created | 4,909 / 4,751 = 1.03 | 10,370 / 10,595 = 0.98 | 12,404 / 11,328 = 1.09 |
| maybe-stack hits | 903 / 905 = 1.00 | 7,926 / 7,516 = 1.05 | 1,030 / 1,032 = 1.00 |
| active-mapper cache hits | 1,395 / 1,383 = 1.01 | 10,953 / 11,184 = 0.98 | 6,995 / 6,861 = 1.02 |
| literal types | 33,730 / 33,277 = 1.01 | 75,128 / 73,995 = 1.02 | 174,968 / 173,980 = 1.01 |
| union types | 8,232 / 8,209 = 1.00 | 24,035 / 24,183 = 0.99 | 28,579 / 28,443 = 1.00 |
| signatures | 31,175 / 31,188 = 1.00 | 73,817 / 76,357 = 0.97 | 132,642 / 132,669 = 1.00 |
| relation cache misses, `recursiveTypeRelatedTo` | 27,031 / 27,070 = 1.00 | 109,148 / 116,600 = 0.94 | 123,163 / 123,746 = 1.00 |

| `getUnionType` calls with 2+ inputs / union front cache lookups | 63,713 / 63,609 | 122,348 / 125,438 | 272,668 / 271,465 |

- **No port divergence of note.** Every count of shared work is within a few percent, and where Track O differs it mostly does less (playwright's instantiations are a TypeScript 7 difference on tsrs's side).
- The ratios above 1: `keyof` types 2.7–6.6×, but at most 1,771 types; checker symbols 1.29–1.56× (tsrs's lazy members, known from TG1); relation-cache hits 1.06–1.21× (more lookups that hit, not more relations).
- Track O-only entry counts (playwright single): `getReducedType` 6.67 M, `getApparentType` 4.26 M, `getPropertyOfType` 1.62 M, `getTypeOfSymbol` 1.39 M, `checkExpression` 0.93 M, `isRelatedTo` 0.39 M, `getFlowTypeOfReference` 0.31 M, `structuredTypeRelatedTo` 0.11 M, `inferFromTypes` 87 K, `resolveCall` 44 K.
- tsrs's memo statistics on the same runs: union front cache hit rate 38 % (bluesky), 69 % (playwright), 43 % (sentry); inference memo 57 / 246 / 1,063 hits (1.4 K / 8.8 K / 26.8 K `inferFromTypes` steps skipped), negligible here.
- So the single-threaded gap is cost per operation, not more operations.

**Applicable list** (tsrs's change, its gain on tsrs's corpus, status in Track O, estimate and effort here):

| tsrs change (note) | tsrs gain | Track O | here |
|---|---|---|---|
| named-union membership through one set (hillclimb r1) | −11.3 % | the cost it removed is `compareTypes` binary searches under TypeScript 7's structural order; TypeScript 6.0.3 sorts unions by type id, `containsType` is an int binary search | no counterpart worth porting |
| union front cache, long keys (hillclimb r1, `perf-union-inference.md`) | −7 % / −0.5…−1.1 % | not present | ported as step 2, no gain, rejected |
| `compareTypes` searches, galloping merge (r1) | −3.2 % | N/A (id order) | — |
| memo bits in `ObjectFlags`: `shouldNormalizeIntersection`, `isWeakType` (r1) | −1.9 %, −1.4 % | not present | intersections are rare here (1.6–18.7 K `getIntersectionType` calls single); `isWeakType` is a short loop over resolved members; < 0.3 % est., small effort; not done |
| IA memo (r1) | −1.3 % | not present | 11–41 K `getIndexedAccessType` calls single; < 0.3 % est., medium effort; not done |
| a name's literal type only when the type has index infos (r1) | −1.0 %, −0.6 % | — | **not exact for TypeScript 6.0.3**: it skips `getStringLiteralType`'s creation; a literal created later gets a later id, which changes union member order |
| relater: one test for two structured types, unrelated regular literals, keys without the key builder (r1) | −0.4 %, −1.5 %, −0.35 % | keys are ints for non-generic pairs already (CO1) | `isRelatedTo` 86–472 K calls single; < 0.2 % est. |
| inline fast paths: `getApparentType`, `getReducedApparentType` for a plain object type, `getNormalizedType` (r1) | part of −38.7 % | not present | `getReducedType` and `getApparentType` are the most called functions → step 3 |
| borrowed lists, presized tables, pooled lists (r1, r4, round 4) | 0.1–0.8 % each | mostly done in RS1, AR1, AR2 | — |
| contextual union constituents skipped per (union, name) (r2) | −1.6 % | not present | built for contextual unions of 8+ members; medium-high effort; not done |
| `checkExpressionForMutableLocation` takes `isConstContext`'s contextual type (r2) | −1.3 % | — | **not exact for TypeScript 6.0.3**: the second `getContextualType` can create types (non-fixing mapper inferences, widened literals), so skipping it moves type ids and union order |
| maybe-stack scan up to 32 keys (r2) | −0.8 % | done (RS1 be7fdc9) | — |
| discriminant property types, `propertiesRelatedTo` reuse, property lookups without a call, `maybeTypeOfKind` OR, recursion identities, `removeSubtypes` key property (r2, r3) | 0.1–1.0 % each | not present | lower relation volume here; each < 0.3 % est. |
| `resolveName` pass-through ancestors (r4) | −0.36 % | done (RS2 bda23e7) | — |
| symbol tables, symbol name hashes, `repr(C)`, `object_target` (r4, round 4, cpu3) | various | N/A (name ids, records) | — |
| packed instantiation keys (round 4), instantiation key without the key builder (cpu3 change 2) | −1.0 %, −1.2 % | keys are int spans (CO2); a key was still built for a mapper that was not active | step 1 |
| active-mapper caches pooled, visited map cleared to size (cpu-checker 1, 4) | −1.5 %, −1 % | done (`KeyMap.ClearScratch`) | — |
| stored lists instead of copies (cpu-checker 7, 8) | −1.9 % | done (RS1 stored type lists) | — |
| infer memo (`perf-heavy-files-infer-memo.md`) | heavy files | not present | inference walks repeat little here (statistics above); the vscode heavy file is not a tail at 16 checkers (all checkers end within 0.05 s of each other with stealing); not done |
| flow memo (`flowmemo.rs`) | | done (RS2 b956f67) | — |

**Parallel loss** (aot-srv `--pool tsrs`, 16 checkers, against `--single`; tsrs `--check` (16 checkers) against `--single`; 3 runs each, medians; process CPU from the OS, Track O's per-checker times from `NEVERSHARP_POOL_STATS=1`; scratch `PC1/ploss/`; starting driver → final driver):

| | sentry | vscode |
|---|---|---|
| Track O single check | 7.37 → 7.38 s | 29.54 → 29.20 s |
| Track O summed checker time at 16 / single | 1.92× → 1.95× | 1.98× → 1.92× |
| of it the GC pause (stops all 16 checkers) | 0.17 → 0.18 s × 16 | 0.43 → 0.42 s × 16 |
| summed checker time without the pause / single | 1.55× → 1.55× | 1.75× → 1.69× |
| types created at 16 / single: Track O | 1.28× | 1.49× |
| types created at 16 / single: tsrs (counts build) | 1.27× (866,543 / 682,758) | 1.49× (3,210,734 / 2,154,962) |
| Track O per type created, without the pause | 1.21× | 1.17× → 1.13× |
| process CPU at 16 / single: Track O (user) | 1.79× (1.66×) → 1.87× (1.66×) | 1.94× (1.81×) → 1.84× (1.79×) |
| process CPU at 16 / single: tsrs (user), two sets | 1.55× (1.33×), 1.53× (1.34×) | 1.61× (1.47×), 1.71× (1.57×) |

- Both sides duplicate the same work at 16 checkers (1.27–1.28× and 1.49× the types).
- **Track O loses more per unit of work:** without the pause each checker is still 13–21 % slower per type created than the single checker; the GC pause adds 0.17–0.43 s to each of the 16 checkers (12–20 % of the summed time). tsrs's user CPU grows about as much as its duplicated work, so its per-unit cost stays nearly flat (as `notes/perf-memory-traffic-32.md` found at 32 checkers).
- Likely causes: the Server GC pause, which stops every checker; and memory traffic of the larger, reference-rich heap shared by 16 checkers (type records plus side arrays, link stores, dictionaries, against tsrs's compact arenas), with card marking and write barriers on stores into the heap. Not separated further (no hardware counters here).

**Order of the steps** (expected gain per effort): step 1 (the new-mapper key: trivial, 73–84 % of instantiations); step 2 (the union front cache: tsrs's largest memo that maps, 38–69 % hit rate); step 3 (inline `getReducedApparentType`: the most called functions); step 4 (the closure per constituent in the named-union test).

### Steps

After each code step the dumps of the hard constraints were identical with the starting driver's: default pool `--dump-diagnostics` on sentry, bluesky and tuning-check; `--pool tsrs` aot-ws on vscode and sentry (identical at the first run every time); default pool `--dump-emit` on playwright and tldraw. A kept step passed Gate C `--sample 200`. Ratios are new against the previous kept step (6 rounds; repeats in brackets).

| step | change | tsrs source | exactness | counted work removed (single) | srv-tsrs sentry, vscode | single ws bluesky, playwright | single srv bluesky, playwright | guards (ws-tsrs sentry, vscode; srv-tsrs tldraw; ws default vscode) | result |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `instantiateTypeWithAlias`: no cache key and lookup for a mapper that was not active | `checker_11.rs` `instantiate_type_with_alias`, `notes/perf-checker-cpu3.md` change 2 | a mapper pushed now gets an empty cache (a new or cleared `KeyMap`) and nothing is stored in it for this call, so the lookup can only miss; same calls, counts, order and result | key build and lookup for 45,657 / 143,422 / 168,554 instantiations (bluesky / playwright / sentry; 73–84 %) | 0.984 [0.957, 1.024], 1.015 [0.973, 1.086] (10 rounds: 0.995 [0.968, 1.023]) | 0.984 [0.978, 0.989], 0.993 [0.988, 0.998] | 0.997 [0.984, 1.011], 1.003 [0.998, 1.008] | 1.015, 0.980, 1.055 [0.968, 1.112] (10 rounds: 1.017 [0.994, 1.058]), **0.993 [0.990, 0.999]** | kept, 4a13dbb |
| 2 | union front cache: a direct-mapped table (2^10 slots per checker) in front of `getUnionType` for 2–8 inputs without alias or origin, tsrs's store rule plus unchanged symbol and signature counts, a shadow mode (`NEVERSHARP_UNION_CACHE=shadow`: no difference on bluesky, playwright and sentry single, vscode and tldraw `--pool tsrs`) | `unioncache.rs`, `notes/perf-union-inference.md` | stored only from calls that created nothing but the union they return, instantiated nothing, took no impure reduction and did not return `errorType`; such a call is a function of its inputs and of grow-only caches | 38–69 % of the union calls (tsrs's hit rates) | 1.016 [0.970, 1.069], 0.996 [0.959, 1.037] | **1.008 [1.005, 1.022]**, 1.014 [0.868, 1.053] | 1.003, 0.995 | 1.013, 0.989, 1.003, 0.999 | rejected (`PC1/step2-union-front-cache-rejected.patch`) |
| 3 | `getReducedApparentType` returns a plain object type (flags Object only, not mapped) at once | the inline fast path of `getReducedApparentType` for a plain object type, `notes/perf-icount-hillclimb.md` | such a type is its own reduced type and its own apparent type, so `getReducedType(getApparentType(getReducedType(t)))` returns it unchanged; nothing is created | `getReducedType` 1.90 M → 0.47 M, 6.67 M → 1.05 M, 7.10 M → 2.36 M; `getApparentType` 1.28 M → 0.57 M, 4.26 M → 1.45 M, 5.51 M → 3.14 M (bluesky / playwright / sentry) | 1.008 [0.963, 1.059], 1.017 [0.930, 1.075] (10 rounds: 0.997 [0.958, 1.056], 0.993 [0.963, 1.023]) | 1.006 [0.998, 1.015] (10 rounds: 1.002 [1.000, 1.009]), **0.992 [0.985, 1.000]** | 1.003, **0.985 [0.981, 0.995]** | 0.977, 1.000, 1.006, 0.994 | kept, cf44185 (bluesky ws within noise above 1; jit-ws at full optimization, 8 rounds: 0.998 [0.994, 1.001], so code placement) |
| 4 | `getUnionType`'s named-union test as a loop (no closure per constituent), `addNamedUnions` over the stored list (no copy) | `checker_13.rs` `get_union_type_worker_inner` (iteration in place) | the same `containsType` calls in the same order | one closure per constituent of unions built from named unions | 0.977 [0.955, 1.061], 1.014 [0.990, 1.035] | 0.994, 0.999 | 1.001, 0.996 | not run | rejected on the main workloads (`PC1/step4-named-unions-loop-rejected.patch`) |

Notes:
- **Step 2:** the cache costs as much as it saves here. TypeScript 6.0.3's uncached path is cheap (insertion by type id, int-span keys in a `KeyMap`), where tsrs's saved a structural sort; and many repeats return `errorType` on these error-rich repositories (tsrs: 29,385 of 63,609 lookups on bluesky), which are never stored.
- Three candidates in a row did not fail (steps 2 and 4 failed, 3 was kept), but nothing left on the list is estimated above 1 % and exact for TypeScript 6.0.3, so the module stopped after step 4.

**What is left, by the survey.** No call-count divergence; no tsrs memo or shortcut left that is above 1 % here and exact for TypeScript 6.0.3's id-ordered unions (the two that map best, the contextual-type reuse and the index-literal shortcut, skip type creations and would move type ids). The gap is per-operation cost, spread evenly (flat profiles in TG1, RS1, RS2 and here), and the parallel loss above: the GC pause that stops all 16 checkers and the memory traffic of the heap. Those point at the memory-model rework (arenas for the type, symbol and signature records), not at further instruction cuts.

## MM1: the checker's memory model as tsrs keeps it (2026-11-06)

Brief: [modules/MM1.md](modules/MM1.md) (author's decision of 2026-11-06). One attempt (Opus 5.5, medium effort, alone, directly on main); commits 49d1fdd (step 2), 60479d2 (step 3), bef64d3 (step 5), 2867a5a (step 8), 2fe9c1d (step 9) and this entry. Starting drivers built from af5e773 into scratch `MM1/base/` (aot-ws, aot-srv, jit-ws). **Every number is exploratory:** interleaved rounds with AR2's `inter2.mjs` and `cmp2.mjs` (scratch `MM1/run.sh`, `step.sh`; `mkv.mjs` is PC1's), the previous kept step against the new one, 6 rounds after 1 warm-up (10–12 for repeats, 7 for the final table), `bootstrapMedianRatio`. Main workloads: sentry and vscode `--check --pool tsrs` with Server GC (16 checkers, `tsrs-check` and `tsgo7-check` in the same rounds) and with Workstation GC (12 checkers); guards: bluesky and playwright `--check --single` (ws), tldraw `--pool tsrs` (srv), vscode `--check` with the default pool (ws), playwright `--check --emit --single` (final table). Builds with `WarningsNotAsErrors=NU1903` (not committed). **No full gate**, by the author's decision.

### Step 0: the probe

**How the collector was switched off.** A gen0 budget alone does not do it: `DOTNET_GCgen0size` of 1, 4 or 16 GB still gave 15 collections on sentry (the budget is capped; every collection is then a gen1 or gen2 one, and the ws pause stayed at 0.63 s). So a scratch driver (`MM1/nogc-probe.patch`, never committed) calls `GC.TryStartNoGCRegion` after the pool is built (`MM1_NOGC=<MB>`, optionally `MM1_NOGC_LOH`), marks that as a phase of its own, and leaves the region open (ending it makes the next allocation collect the whole 9 GB heap). Server GC left the region in about half the runs while a background collection was running; the parallel no-GC variants therefore also set `DOTNET_gcConcurrent=0`, with a control variant (`normal-nc`: the base driver with only that setting; it is 3–13 % slower than normal, all in the front end's collections). Region sizes: sentry 8 GB (srv, + 8 GB large objects) or 4 GB, vscode 40 GB (srv, + 8 GB) or 16 GB; a run that still left the region is marked and was excluded where it mattered. Scratch `MM1/probe.mjs`, `psum.mjs`, results `MM1/p0/`.

**Item 1, the upper bound without collection.** "Normal with the no-GC check" is the normal run's wall time with its check phase replaced by the no-GC run's check phase of the same round (front end and exit unchanged; the region start itself, 0.01–2 s of committing, is not counted). 5 rounds (vscode 3; vscode srv 5):

**Item 3, the parallel loss and the memory-traffic part** (PC1's method: summed per-checker check time at N checkers against the single check, per type created; same rounds):

| | summed / single | types N / single | per type created | per type, without the pause |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| sentry srv 16, normal | 1.85× | 1.29× | 1.441× | 1.188× (pause 0.15 s × 16) |
| sentry srv 16, no GC | 1.44× | 1.28× | **1.123×** | |
| sentry ws 12, normal / no GC | 2.06× / 1.28× | 1.25× | 1.654× / **1.030×** | |
| vscode srv 16, normal | 1.94× | 1.49× | 1.298× | 1.141× |
| vscode srv 16, no GC | 1.66× | 1.49× | **1.113×** | |
| vscode ws 12, normal / no GC | 1.94× / 1.37× | 1.38× | 1.405× / **0.993×** | |

- With the collector off, the per-type loss is 11–12 % at 16 checkers and 0–3 % at 12. The machine has 12 cores (24 logical CPUs): 16 checkers share cores, 12 do not. So most of what is left at 16 checkers is the cores' sharing (tsrs has it too: PC1 measured its per-unit CPU at 16 checkers at about 1.05×/0.99×), and the part that dense records could address is at most the difference, about 6 % at 16 checkers and nothing at 12.

**Item 2, the census of what a collection marks** (AR1's tools: the AR probe applied for the census and removed again, `dotnet-trace` of the jit-ws driver with `DOTNET_GCgen0size=2000000`, `AR1/acc`; scratch `MM1/tr/`, `MM1/dump/`):
- **Per collection in the check** (means by generation; the trace cannot count cards, so "cards" is the time of the stage that marks from older generations, and the bytes it reached):
  - vscode `--pool tsrs` ws: gen0 82 collections × 8.62 ms (stacks 0.55, cards 2.15, plan/relocate/compact 5.65 ms), gen1 51 × 18.99 ms (0.60, 3.34, 14.83 ms) = 1.68 s; through cards 238 MB (gen0) and 524 MB (gen1) of the promoted bytes, through stacks 3 and 24 MB.
  - sentry ws: gen0 16 × 9.18 ms (0.40, 2.25, 6.29), gen1 19 × 20.1 ms (0.47, 3.23, 15.9) = 0.53 s.
  - So the cards are about a quarter of a collection, the stacks 5 %, the rest is planning, relocating and copying; AR1's fit and AR2 showed that this part follows the number of objects (the dead ones of gen0 included), not the bytes.
- **Promoted in the check:** vscode 1,209 MB, reference-free 1,013 MB: the mapper store's type chunks 459 MB (38 %), strings 205 MB (17 %), `Int32[]` 129 MB (11 %), mapper records 87 MB (7 %); with references 137 MB: `InferenceContext` 44 MB, diagnostics 25 MB, relation cache entries 14 MB, `List<Type>` 10 MB, `SymTable`-holding dictionaries 7 MB. sentry 319 MB: strings 63 MB (20 %), `Int32[]` 55 MB, diagnostics 54 MB (17 %), `Type[]` 26 MB.
- **Survivors at the end of the check** (sentry, forced full collection, `dumpheap -stat`): 1.37M objects, 855 MB; with references 498K objects / 317 MB. By count: strings 664K, `ProgramDiagnostic` 239K, `InferenceContext` and its `InferenceInfo[]` 16K each, `Type[]` 13K. Large arrays with references: object type record chunks 116 MB (131 arrays), transient symbol record chunks 72 MB (219; references only through `SymTable`'s store field), symbol-table entry chunks 65 MB, index info chunks 21 MB, signature records 15 MB, expression links 11 MB, literal records 6 MB. vscode: `dotnet-dump collect` failed ("Value does not fall within the expected range", as in AR2); AR1's vscode census stands (1.59M strings, 325K inference contexts, 103K diagnostics, 95K `Type[]`, 94K `List<Type>`).
- **Strings:** a probe counted that 74 % (vscode) and 92 % (sentry) of the name table's 747K / 193K names are read as strings, and 90 % / 95 % of the 350K / 129K regular string literal values; a text arena with strings made on demand would save few of them.

**Stop rule:** not triggered: the upper bound with Server GC is 16 % on sentry (0.839) and about 7 % on vscode, and the memory-traffic loss at 16 checkers is 11–12 % (of which about 6 % beyond the cores' sharing).

**Plan** (by the census; estimated gain on the ws main workloads): the mapper type chunks (38 % of the promoted bytes, but AR1 and AR2 moved them twice without gain: 0–2 %); inference contexts (650K surviving objects with references: 3–5 %); base type lists (190K: 1–2 %); the records with references (`SymTable`'s store field in the transient symbol, object type and union records; composite signatures; index info components: 1–3 %); the type record array out of the large-object heap (1 %); relation and name caches without references (1 %); strings (the largest count, but read as strings: not planned); dense records (at most the 6 % above, at 16 checkers only; not planned).

### Steps

After each code step the dumps of the hard constraints were identical with the starting driver's: default pool `--dump-diagnostics` on sentry, bluesky and tuning-check; `--pool tsrs` ws on vscode and sentry (a second run was needed for vscode in steps 5, 7 and 8, as AR2 allows); default pool `--dump-emit` on playwright and tldraw. A kept step passed Gate C `--sample 200`. Ratios are new against the previous kept step (6 rounds; repeats in brackets); pause: vscode ws median.

| step | group (census share) | tsrs source | change | sentry srv, vscode srv | sentry ws, vscode ws | guards: bluesky, playwright single; tldraw srv; vscode default | pause vscode ws | result |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| 2 | inference contexts (325K + 325K surviving objects with references on vscode) | `checker.rs` `InferenceContext` behind a `P` pointer; `mem-checker-scratch.md` | `InferenceContext` as a handle to a reference-free record in the `CheckerArena`; its inferences an arena block (`InferenceInfos`) instead of an array; the comparer an index of a per-checker comparer table; `inferredTypeParameters` and the intra-expression sites slots of per-checker side tables (a cleared site list frees its slot); the fixing and non-fixing mappers hold the record's address instead of an object-table slot | 0.974 [0.939, 1.016], 0.986 [0.953, 1.013] | 1.020 (10 rounds: **0.955 [0.927, 0.984]**), **0.953 [0.918, 0.976]** | 0.985, 0.990; 1.004; 0.984 | 1.93 → 1.63 s | kept, 49d1fdd |

| 10 | the string literal cache (`Dictionary<string, Type>`) | `mem-checker-heap.md` | the same table kind, the slot's text being its literal type's value | 1.003, 0.990 | 0.997, 1.014 [0.992, 1.027] | 1.001, 0.999; 0.986; 0.997 | 1.29 → 1.28 s | rejected (`MM1/step10-string-literal-cache-rejected.patch`) |

Notes:
- **Exactness.** Steps 2 and 3 keep the same elements in the same order and make the same calls (a context's inferences are replaced in place by `mergeInferences` as before; a base type push stores a copy with the element appended, and nothing holds the old list while a type's base types are resolved); steps 5, 8 and 9 change only how a table or a cache finds its entries: equality of tables is still the identity of the Map, the relation key packing is injective and never equals a text key, the name ids are given in the same order. No type, symbol or signature is created in another order; nothing is shared between checkers (the registry of step 5 is process-wide, but each slot belongs to one checker's store, and a `SymTable` of one checker never reaches another).
- **Memory safety.** Context records and inference blocks are blocks of the checker's `CheckerArena`, which lives as long as the checker (its stores reference it) and frees everything in its finalizer; a mapper record holds a context's address only inside the same checker. A registry slot is freed only when the checker that owns it is unreachable, so no live `SymTable` names a freed slot; the registry holds no reference to the checker (the `SlotOwner` is not reachable from the store), so the store and the checker can be collected.
- **What did not pay:** moving reference-free chunks out of the collected heap (steps 1 and 6, AR1 step 2, AR2 steps 2 and 7); an extra indirection on the type record (step 4: +7 % on sentry srv). What paid: removing objects and entries with references that survive (steps 2, 3, 5) and replacing dictionaries by reference-free tables with cheaper lookups (steps 8, 9: these also made the single-threaded runs 1–1.5 % faster).
- Steps 5, 8 and 9 were kept on the 10- and 12-round repeats of the Server GC main workloads whose 6-round median was above 1 (as AR2 did for sentry ws); step 9's sentry srv median is above 1 at 6 and 10 rounds and below at 12, all within noise: kept under the within-noise clause (it removes 750K entries with references on vscode and the ws pause).

### Census after the last step (step 9)

AR probe drivers built from 2fe9c1d with the probe added and removed again (scratch `MM1/census9/`), the same trace and accounts as in step 0:
- **Per collection in the check, vscode ws:** gen0 84 × 6.20 ms (stacks 0.54, cards 1.55, after-mark 3.92 ms; step 0: 8.62 ms), gen1 46 × 15.13 ms (0.58, 2.29, 12.04 ms; step 0: 18.99 ms) = 1.22 s (step 0 1.68 s). sentry ws: gen0 16 × 8.02 ms (cards 1.85), gen1 19 × 18.34 ms (cards 2.95) = 0.48 s (0.53 s).
- **Promoted in the check:** vscode 1,080 MB (step 0 1,209), with references 58 MB (137): the mapper type chunks 443 MB (41 %), strings 189 MB (18 %), `Int32[]` 114 MB, mapper records 84 MB, diagnostics 21 MB, string-keyed literal caches 16 MB. sentry 340 MB: strings 87 MB (26 %), `Int32[]` 51 MB, diagnostics 49 MB (15 %), `Type[]` 39 MB.
- **Survivors at the end of the check, sentry:** 1.32M objects, 836 MB (step 0: 1.37M, 855 MB); with references 473K objects / 224 MB (498K / 317 MB). The transient symbol chunks (58 MB) and the relation tables hold no references now; inference contexts are gone from the heap. Left with references: `ProgramDiagnostic` 239K and their lists, the object type record chunks (113 MB; tuple element flags and labels), index info chunks (21 MB, components), signature records (15 MB, composite signatures), expression links (11 MB), literal records (6 MB, the values). vscode: `dotnet-dump` failed again.

**What is left:**
1. **Strings** (664K surviving on sentry, 1.59M on vscode; 18–26 % of the promoted bytes): names and literal values, 74–95 % of which are read as strings. A text arena pays only once their readers compare and hash spans; that is a rework of the 160 `NameOf` sites and the 40 literal value readers.
2. **Diagnostics** (239K on sentry, 15 % of its promoted bytes): `ProgramDiagnostic` objects with their message strings, a type the program layer and the dump writers share.
3. **The collections' count and their planning**, which follows the number of young objects (4.5 GB small-object allocation per vscode check), not the memory model.
4. **Dense records** would address at most about 6 % at 16 checkers (the loss beyond the cores' sharing) and nothing at 12; step 4 showed how sensitive the hot type reads are to the layout.
5. The remaining records with references (composite signatures, index info components, tuple arrays, literal values) cost nothing measurable when moved (step 7).

**Gate C:** `--sample 200`, 5 runs of 15: after steps 2, 3, 5, 8 and 9 (all pass, `{"track":"optimized","phase":"checker","modes":["types"],"sections":["diagnostics","types","symbols"],"sample":200,"pass":true}`). Gate D and the full Gate C were not run (author's decision).

**Rules:** C# edited with the file tools only. Bent or to note: (1) the step 0 probes (the no-GC region in `CheckMode.cs`; counters of names and string literal values read, with a `NoAlloc` attribute removed for the time of the probe) were written with the file tools and removed with `git checkout`; AR1's probe hooks were applied with `git apply` and its `ArProbe.cs.txt` copied into the tree with `cp` for each census, then removed with `git checkout` and `rm`; step 1 applied AR2's saved patch with `git apply`; rejected steps were undone with `git checkout` after saving their diff; to build step 2's jit-ws driver, step 3's files were set aside with `git stash push -- src` and restored with `git stash pop` (version-control operations and copies, no text edits by script); (2) the no-GC probe used `GC.TryStartNoGCRegion` in a scratch driver and `DOTNET_gcConcurrent=0` for the parallel no-GC runs (with a control variant), and its upper bound replaces the check phase in the normal run instead of comparing whole runs (the region start and the non-concurrent front end would otherwise be counted); (3) the per-collection census gives means per generation, and the cards are reported as the card-marking stage's time and reached bytes (the trace has no card counts); the vscode survivor census failed (`dotnet-dump`), only sentry's was taken; `dotnet-dump` attached to the census driver this module started; (4) step 3 was kept under the code-placement exception (playwright single AOT 1.016, JIT 0.999); steps 5, 8 and 9 on 10/12-round repeats of Server GC rows whose 6-round medians were 1.003–1.015; step 9 within noise; (5) `sed` was used on scratch scripts only (copies of AR2's trace and heap scripts, one probe parameter), never on a source file.
